Treat these as\n        # month-end entries, retaining their month for payroll grouping.\n        next_month = date(year + (month == 12), month % 12 + 1, 1)\n        return next_month - timedelta(days=1)\n\n\ndef _month_starts(first_day: date, last_day: date, weekday: int) -> list[date]:\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"First occurrence of weekday in each calendar month across the data.\\\"\\\"\\\"\n    starts: list[date] = []\n    year, month = first_day.year, first_day.month\n    while (year, month) <= (last_day.year, last_day.month):\n        month_first = date(year, month, 1)\n        offset = (weekday - month_first.weekday()) % 7\n        starts.append(month_first + timedelta(days=offset))\n        if month == 12:\n            year, month = year + 1, 1\n        else:\n            month += 1\n    return starts\n\n\ndef answer() -> dict[str, list[int]]:\n    employees = _read_csv('employees.csv')\n    tasks = _read_csv('tasks.csv')\n    timesheet = _read_csv('timesheet.csv')\n\n    rates = {row['PESEL']: Decimal(row['hourly_rate']) for row in employees}\n    task_pay = {str(row['task_id']): Decimal(row['pay_per_completion']) for row in tasks}\n    roster_ids = list(rates)\n\n    # The invalid timesheet PESELs are single records for roster employees with\n    # small, unique digit differences.
